## 2. Volume overview and narrative architecture

V15 turns a threat to the party’s ordinary home into a question about whether that home should continue on the same terms. The first movement confronts an infiltrator who can make the town’s dependence on help into actual dependence on her. Kazuma’s first answer is a private safety bargain. Aqua’s rejection then makes detached prudence personally intolerable, and he begins a costly, ugly campaign that neither pure civic heroism nor pure selfishness adequately describes. The middle movement places that campaign’s agent under genuine altered allegiance. The resulting servant does not become an empty mechanism: domination removes restraints while leaving enough inference, initiative, appetite and literal-minded accounting to make him disastrously expensive to his captor. That emergent sabotage benefits the town, but the narration does not establish a secret voluntary resistance plan.

The final movement starts after the officer has actually been weakened and handed over. An honest heavenly replacement cannot reproduce Aqua’s recruitment success, so returning the troublesome goddess now has institutional value. A familiar call to defeat the king becomes her personally urgent route home. The novel successively rejects an easy extermination shortcut, an easy account that she simply does not value her friends, and an easy account that Kazuma only values her utility. Wiz’s willing trust stops a purification that ordinary opposition might have intensified. Kazuma first wants her to stay, then recognizes that the life she lost was not his to dismiss. His eventual willingness to consider a route and the final intent to pursue her are bounded changes in commitment; no mission, search or military resolution is completed by the principal ending.

The architecture makes domestic repetition do causal work. Both endpoints begin with waking/eating routines rather than world-saving spectacle. A water-purified condiment, a stolen fish and a conspicuously unpeaceful claim of peace define the familiar household before its goddess becomes socially unnecessary. Repeated confinement, arrest, debt and food reappear at larger stakes: a safe hut becomes a toilet trap, a devotee becomes a household budget catastrophe, a rare cure becomes someone’s treasured gift, and delivering dinner becomes an unobtrusive way to maintain contact. Sleep moves from desired leisure to escape from death, then to an insomnia that exposes attachment. Dawn/noon jokes remain, but not every inability to sleep is merely a gag.

The seven admitted units have distinct functions. Pr stages ordinary friction and the first demonstration that successful purification can defeat the user’s actual preference. C1 uncovers the rival’s methods and builds the conflict between Kazuma’s safety calculation and his response to Aqua’s exclusion. C2 makes solitary harassment effective enough to expose gratitude/debt mechanics, then uses a relaxed apparent settlement to compromise the investigator himself. C3’s dated diary lets the altered man document the catastrophe from inside his own self-approving reasoning; a live return-and-cure scene then restores a different evaluative state before the still-dangerous captive kills him. C4 resolves that local crisis through revival, actual reset and institutional handoff, then opens the larger return-home conflict and a parallel Crimson defense summons. F tests Aqua’s shortcut against Wiz’s friendship and develops a quiet balcony conversation whose sincerity survives its insults. Ep delivers the absent goddess’s letter and changes Kazuma’s posture from deliberating in bed to immediate pursuit intention.

The form also separates kinds of access. Serena supplies intelligence about earlier corpse staging, robot control and intended attacks; these are newly available reports, not permission to rewrite V14’s original evidence states. The diary contains reports of conduct and a clearly invented erotic passage, so quoted speech there is not automatically actual speech by the depicted woman. The afterlife supplies direct conversation with Eris, who relays the party’s ground-side aftermath through an attributed report; the source does not establish her observation mechanism. Aqua’s written calls convey unseen body interference, while the returned narrator sees only guilty behavior and disordered clothing. The closing letter is an attributed document with self-serving allegations, hyperbole and erased writing. Those devices are evidence filters, not decorations that can be ignored when scoring causes.

The strongest emotional turns interrupt familiar scripts without abolishing the personalities. Megumin and Darkness trust a leader who has withheld the relevant facts. Darkness supports release despite discomfort with influence, then refuses to guarantee him again after he publicly humiliates her. Megumin uses voluntary intimacy and physical restraint together during the cure, later supports Yunyun’s desired town defense while also reacting to her own reserve status. Aqua is both powerful and unable to force the acknowledgment or return route she wants; Wiz’s acceptance changes what Aqua can bring herself to choose. Kazuma can acknowledge genuine care and still convert it into insults, privacy defenses, manipulative reasoning or inappropriate touch. The volume makes those competing motives inspectable rather than resolving them into a single virtue adjective.

## 3. Chapter-by-chapter analytical reading

### 3.1. Prologue — ordinary peace is a viewpoint

The breakfast premise separates capacity from result immediately. Aqua’s accidental contact can purify soy sauce; she first denies touching it, invokes the companions’ long association and then actually eats the watery consequence before requesting salt (PrP0008–0019). No inability to purify is demonstrated. The trouble is unwanted activation, material choice and refusal to concede. Chomusuke’s fish theft turns nourishment into a hierarchy dispute. Claws and the heat cry precede Aqua’s report that the creature breathed fire; Kazuma does not turn to observe that moment, and says he had already reported its fish-cooking behavior (P0021–0031). Aqua’s confident recognition performance then depends on covert glances for confirmation. Kazuma explicitly lacks a certain identity himself (P0033–0038). The smell report that it does not smell demonic and instead smells of soap is a bounded observation, not a certified species reveal.

Bargaining and retreat replace the announced confrontation when the robe is attacked (P0038–0041). Kazuma’s 「平和だなあ」 is thus an evaluation of familiar commotion from a protected meal, immediately contradicted by Aqua’s rescue appeal (P0043–0044). The scene permits a real shared-home comfort claim without requiring that all participants experience the same event as harmless or pleasant. That distinction becomes consequential when later distress no longer feels routine to him.

### 3.2. Chapter 1 — a private bargain becomes personally intolerable

The allegedly unbeatable officer is currently crouching in an empty lot after a colleague has taken her money (C1P0006–0008). Her tobacco demand and rough exposed persona produce involuntary deference in Kazuma, though he deals with Dust differently (P0012–0018). She accepts the description of her god as evil rather than claiming universal religious righteousness (P0022–0030). Her account that cemetery bodies were puppets, and that the beautiful cursed-girl story was a reusable pacifying fabrication, arrives here as her admission (P0034–0043). It explains earlier anomalies prospectively in the sequence; it does not convert the V14 reader into someone who knew the mechanism then.

Their interview makes the villain momentarily the audience for a protagonist’s moral deficiency. Kazuma instantly agrees he is a NEET with no wish to risk his life for humanity and offers only remote sympathy for strangers. His defense that he is an ordinary Japanese man visibly loses confidence while Serena finds his lack of hesitation exceptional (P0046–0064). He then enjoys being credited with strategic importance, privately panics at the timing of stopped cheat arrivals and reassures himself with an unobserved competent replacement angel (P0066–0094). The triumphant moral speech volunteers an unknown sexual reputation and mistakes recruitment for admiration of his power (P0095–0108). Neither the refusal of sexual temptation nor his self-description as steel-minded establishes a calm low-arousal baseline: agitation is narrated alongside refusal (P0122–0126).

He accepts noninterference and secrecy in exchange for safety, wraps that choice in concern for the town, and lends the bankrupt officer money (P0127–0138). His following private account credits his companions’ actual contributions and acknowledges that he once made an enemy stronger; the practical motive is fear of a fight he cannot win alone (P0143–0153). At home he tries closing the door on sobbing Aqua and is pulled back into responsibility. Her guild ban and bill are reports; alcohol purification is his inference here (P0155–0170). Megumin’s card/reward reasoning and Darkness’s information network are independently useful, even though he orders them to stop investigating because he already possesses dangerous concealed information (P0174–0180).

He brings Darkness as backup, while her clothes and later embarrassment reveal a different expectation of the invitation (P0185–0232). The public consultation scene gives a precise limit to the rival’s apparent universal service: romance charms attract clients, but a Crimson girl’s wish for dinner company is refused (P0210–0223; later identified as Yunyun at C2P0225–0226). A toothpick test directly supports reflected injury, whereas the city-wide lethal aftermath of Serena’s death remains her threat (P0236–0253). Kazuma’s dispersal of the crowd teasing Darkness shows local protection without curing his willingness to expose her elsewhere (P0257–0264).

The injured adventurer asks for Aqua (P0308). She promises and physically approaches to treat, but followers block her before any Aqua healing cast is shown; after Serena’s repeated Heal, the patient himself rejects Aqua’s supplementary attempt (P0310–0346). This is denied access, not failed healing execution or demonstrated lack of readiness. The narrator’s comparison of normal reciprocal priest/warrior work with sudden one-sided reverence is a causal question, and puppetry remains his explicit hypothesis at P0363. Aqua’s unusual withdrawal changes his appraisal even while he lists her familiar faults and denies romantic feeling (P0350–0385). The friends’ trust and silent contact intensify the burden because they do not know the bargain’s facts (P0389–0439).

Serena grants a restricted solo-opposition exception as repayment of the cash loan, confident that his prudence will prevent him using it (P0410–0415). He has already considered protective tactics before Aqua asks whether she is an unnecessary goddess (P0395–0401/P0468). The later commands therefore mark a new commitment to act within ongoing care, not the first appearance of any care or practical planning. He requests buffs and specifies healing, police transfer and crowd handling before launching the kick (P0472–0486). Darkness objects before the strike; Megumin’s excitement follows the decision (P0493–0500). The apparent heroic declaration deliberately accompanies a legally actionable, self-injuring assault. At P0505 the kick is shown; incapacitation and healing consequences are not yet established within this chapter.

### 3.3. Chapter 2 — effective improvisation, bad social accounts and genuine capture

Interrogation sets institutional knowledge against absurd cover stories. The chief recognizes both the suspect’s earlier contributions and reason to conceal the rival’s secret, while he requests a lawyer and threatens a fabricated harassment accusation (C2P0007–0024). The reflected kick did incapacitate him; his present lack of injuries is observed, but Aqua’s healing him is still his inference (P0035–0038). Darkness’s response to his silence is not the rigid refusal both he and the officer expect. She asks release, offers aftermath responsibility and receives his grateful embrace (P0064–0085). No complaint exists at this point, qualifying the legal situation (P0073). The officer’s idealized image of the unmarried noble then prompts Kazuma to weaponize true private history, ending in the kiss admission (P0087–0107). Literal truth does not erase its humiliating purpose or Darkness’s subsequent refusal to guarantee him again (P0110/P0373).

The campaign’s first public intervention is an act of deliberate social sabotage. Serena’s ordinary good-person reassurance has actually cheered a rejected man before Kazuma volunteers her as his girlfriend, then treats her explicit refusal as coded desire and incites imposed handholding (P0135–0170). His helper framing is not independent evidence that the stranger benefits. Technical care and impulsive miscalculation coexist in the cup shot: he checks range, wind and nonlethal arrow shape, hits the intended object and learns that unknown hot coffee reflects injury indirectly too (P0177–0214). Yunyun’s criminality concern is real before the uniquely-relied-on appeal recruits emphatic participation and precise stealth support. A secrecy request then exposes her lack of confidants (P0217–0241). The prolonged mana-funded ice sealing traps Serena without a toilet while Kazuma eats shaved ice (P0242–0260); this is not an effortless universal combat spell.

Serena’s public mask frays under further preparation and slander, then snaps back for a passing acquaintance (P0268–0305). When force threatens, Kazuma exploits raised prayer hands, robe geometry and special wire to prevent spell release while exposing her lower body (P0315–0356). Actual brief follower lucidity and renewed erotic reverence are observed; later street sales and a new complaint are reported (P0338–0347/P0366–0375). The chief’s crude hypotheses and his own comparison of healing, thanks and Yunyun’s unfulfilled consultation help him approach a debt mechanism, but it is not yet omniscient knowledge (P0384–0416). Her interruption provokes an unmarried insult and a flood of ordinary career/family wishes. A costly express letter obtains elite guarantees, and fear-driven remote compliance is misread as romantic influence by a newly deferential officer who then markets herself (P0407–0463).

Aqua’s luminous release welcome turns into competitive arrest taunting. Kazuma’s wish to send her home includes both actual care and resurrection insurance; her demand for sleepovers reflects ignorance of his task (P0465–0500). Once pursuit begins, his account minimizes serious harassment and creates civilian costs by overturning merchandise (P0511–0527). He again considers protecting Aqua before her evil-sense retort triggers a thought of tripping her; the thought is not executed (P0531–0537). Both propose being the first climber in a cooperative wall escape, neither trusting the other. The actual dead-end solution is Vanir’s prepositioned shop, not an executed climbing maneuver (P0538–0558).

The street stall gives several distinct kinds of asymmetry. Kazuma does not know which former colleague would support whom and cannot safely treat friendship as a universal alliance rule (P0574–0585). Vanir sells noninterference for Serena’s exact remaining wallet; the loan counterclaim produces an observed freed follower, followed by sexual gratitude restoring his vacant expression (P0586–0627). The general debt account remains Kazuma’s inference (P0630–0638). Sexual-service blackmail obtains his attention; the seemingly useless potion catalogue contains a real possible cure (P0639–0664). Aqua then executes purification successfully for Wiz’s hydration, destroying the case’s relevant property because she has heard it is useless and does not know the intended sale. Wiz thanks her and reports the holy sting (P0675–0677). The failed merchant, not the allegedly useless deity, is momentarily speechless; Wiz is subsequently seen scorched during the fight, with Kazuma attributing it to possible collateral effects, while both outside observers move toward exhausted settlement (P0681–0713).

The settling investigator thinks the revenge for Aqua is done, then denies that motive repeatedly (P0715–0723). His relaxation supplies the opening for a genuine trap: thanks P0748 is followed by cognitive/motor impairment P0757–0767, while further compulsory answers compound the debt. Serena’s military explanation makes Axel a training/supply bottleneck, not merely a town attacked because of Kazuma’s fame (P0778–0805). These are interested intelligence reports; no attack is yet shown. Her final order to discard restraint/conscience is part of the coercive state change (P0813–0815), not a neutral summary that every subsequent act expresses his ordinary stable choice.

His ensuing loyalty is also not equivalent to loss of all initiative. He asks how to avoid freedom, volunteers unwanted bodily service and interprets intensified control as exceptional esteem (P0842–0884). The source does not show a concealed voluntary counterplan. At the inn, preserving domination becomes a reason to refuse payment; humiliating public charity frees another donor, then he imposes bodily repayment through a debt account Serena herself disputes (P0886–0930). Her discarded-morals order returns as an argument for a shared room and continued upkeep (P0932–0958). Polite obedience and forceful self-interest now coexist under altered allegiance; neither total agency nor total puppet emptiness fits the observed combination.

### 3.4. Chapter 3 — the servant documents the destruction of the master

The dated diary is a controlled-access device: Serena orders it to inspect his mind, and he charges for the obligation and exploits its compulsory reader through erotic fiction (C3P0006–0031). P0029 is an invented scene, not proof of naked contact or an actual utterance by Serena. Other entries report bodily coercion, actual tears, reflected punches, expensive meals and forced work. They are serious evidence of the narrator’s current approval structure, but their first-person diary form remains distinct from a directly staged present scene. The newly reported robot-control failure belongs to this volume’s information boundary (P0044–0045). The two participants’ matching crouches after his retaliatory head punch are the reported result, not evidence he planned to spare her (P0048–0049).

Searching friends become leverage for higher tribute. The diary reports that the dark-priest occupation shown on her card prevents accepting adventurer requests without exposure and explains the missing guild rewards, while his luxury consumption denies her even a share of food under a self-serving loyalty rule (P0061–0075). Borrowed money releases other puppets, sexual recruitment draws police intervention, and the remaining servant resists even liberation by praying to the same god (P0077–0096). A comforting hand ends in a cash-or-body tariff when she actually cries (P0098–0107). Altered state, individual initiative, visible victim distress and repeated refusal cues must all remain in the reconstruction; the beneficial destruction of the infiltration cannot retroactively make these acts a planned protective performance.

The live scene confirms the inconvenient sincere believer through reflected toothpick injury. 「無宗派」 and Serena’s 「無宗教」 are not interchangeable conclusions, and Kazuma himself qualifies his description with the Christmas/year-end bells/Shinto itinerary (P0131–0149). Later myriad-gods (八百万, native ruby やおよろず) and Flying Spaghetti Monster examples explain the cultural mismatch in his casual religious affiliation, without claiming every person in Japan has his exact relationship to faith (P0167–0175). Serena’s strangling hurts her too; his subsequent unwanted skirt lift follows actual distress (P0169–0181). Every order or absence of an order then becomes another alleged debt.

The empty home evokes attraction and attachment, but the blond girl is only a possible perception and is not identified here (P0199–0218). At the guild he belatedly supplies the party’s schedule, eats at Serena’s expense and leaves her washing dishes. At the cemetery he painfully refuses corpse work under an incongruous religious excuse, withholds useful information and resists control reallocation. 「傀儡ポイント」 is his invented label, corrected by Serena’s explanation, not an official quantified interface; the repeated do-not-resist instruction is explicitly compared to a comedian’s advance cue (P0248–0286). These are local technical and social choices by the altered man, not proof that the initial domination was fake.

The return scene exposes an important relational baseline. Megumin and Darkness distinguish his old excuse-based, hesitant harassment from the new brazen version; Aqua says not to return him broken (P0303–0328). Their characterization is evidence of what they recognize and value, not a certificate that his older behavior was always welcome. Aqua guards the gift because she values its sender and does not know its function; her request for information cannot become a knowing refusal to cure him (P0330–0340). Darkness interposes before Death, the spell fails and the priest is captured (P0343–0351). Aqua’s later divided-favor diagnosis explains the weakened priest as attributed expertise (P0387); it is not a preplanned explanation they demonstrably possessed before the failed spell.

Megumin’s voluntary display, competitive claim and physical hold combine personal acceptance with practical restraint (P0370–0405). The first cure approach still fails: one unheld hand Steals and throws the potion, and Aqua saves it. Serena’s command, a tighter hug and Aqua’s face-orientation instruction then enable actual administration (P0416–0443). Do not call the whole procedure flawless or erase its local repair. The released narrator briefly recalls wanting revenge for Aqua P0456, then later cannot explain that wish P0471–0475; both access states remain. His immediate stronger-hug pretext is rejected, confirming that restored familiarity does not dissolve Megumin’s ability to end the contact (P0460–0466).

Disposition remains difficult because he rejects direct/indirect murder and admits partial attachment after cohabitation (P0480–0497). Darkness’s offered killing recruits an actual decision: he chooses a reset he expects to suffer too, weighs relative loss and retains a slow affordable rebuilding option (P0498–0514/P0532). His claim to finish alone cannot erase actual allies, cure labor and Vanir’s supplied item. Darkness’s later reflected pinching uses the captive’s pain for her chosen pleasure despite protests; it is not an automatically protective interrogation (P0516–0528). The priest’s faith is restored to exclusive allocation when Kazuma is cured. Her final Death precedes his reaching hand and Darkness’s attempted gag (P0538–0548); the resulting death is established next chapter, not inferred solely from the spell name.

### 3.5. Chapter 4 — a real resolution does not settle the home question

Eris’s playful maid welcome acknowledges the repetition of the death room, then gives an actual report of Darkness and Serena both losing consciousness and the party’s competent aftermath (C4P0006–0021). Kazuma’s demoralization and growing habituation are real alongside the comedy (P0023–0041). Eye-level comfort turns into marriage teasing, but Eris explicitly denies the suggested jealousy/interruption scheme (P0043–0051). Her good-memory prompt instead recruits his hardship inventory and a rich-pet rebirth fantasy (P0053–0075). Aqua’s noisy blame nearly brings out the actual protective motive, while reports of Megumin interfering with the corpse change his refusal into panic. The first-time expression is initially read as a sexual opportunity, then as bodily violation; its edible object is unnamed (P0085–0122). These are textually represented inter-world calls, not directly observed complete acts. Rumpled trousers, hidden object and overinformative denials corroborate interference after revival without establishing every threatened degree of it (P0124–0137).

The next-day account resolves the officer locally: reset was actually applied, both are level1 and Serena was given to police with the intelligence (P0156–0157). The reduced lethal curse, cancelled attack, countermeasures and bounty have differing evidentiary status: Kazuma expects/inferentially projects some, the chief promises others, and no full campaign or paid reward is shown (P0158–0165). Eris’s staffing explanation is now reported: the honest angel’s complete risk disclosure cannot produce Aqua’s recruitment results (P0176–0188). That is a bounded institutional incentive, not a general moral vindication of deception.

Aqua’s dawn mission request first casts Kazuma as selected hero while admitting he cannot simply win. Her barrier-and-network plan has a real partial rationale; her fallback of solo assassination is rejected (P0144–0230). The lunch reform speech then confuses an allegedly changed character with a genuinely changed goal. Both companions say he has long been idle, she admits wanting his desperate debtor state, and duty language collapses under scrutiny (P0234–0262). Megumin’s casual ultimate-feat appetite and Darkness’s armor-destruction fantasy are distinct values, not shared consent to the danger Kazuma foresees (P0264–0277).

Private hurt about Aqua’s willingness to leave leads him through alternative explanations: rejection-driven unhappiness, divine timescale or simple excitement at being needed again. None is privileged access to her mind (P0279–0296). A tentative willingness to grant the wish stops at the difficulty of the king; secret chosen-hero whispers later provoke a false nocturnal-assault alarm and an explicit confession of attempted brainwashing, not established successful magic (P0298–0318).

Three days after arrest, the royal transfer is planned, not completed (P0323–0324). Kazuma’s equipment sorting recalls the game-item hoarding pattern, then Aqua accidentally provides the wrong survey before a curated one treats nearly every ordinary problem as the king’s fault (P0329–0350). The written responses are attributed documents, not newly verified interviews. The contemplated potion exploit retains both loss of magic and hair; Darkness’s monster-attraction request is separately refused (P0351–0362). The rooster’s sudden growth later receives an ordinary item-use explanation: Aqua gave it the level potion, not a confirmed dragon awakening (P0369–0387/P0417). Her protective drama and proposed delegation of coop labor do not prove a speaking dragon or an already-completed chore.

Aqua also generates a real strategic idea about a depleted castle during the planned offensive. Its success depends on unknown troop/guard behavior, but Kazuma recognizes possible merit (P0393–0409). Her hair-healing answer distinguishes damaged follicles from ordinary loss while misunderstanding the current target (P0410–0412). Stone throwing, wind defense, ineffective water deterrence and then earth create a locally adaptive counter by Kazuma, ending in actual tears and his satisfaction (P0425–0444). Darkness calling it fun does not make it mutually harmless.

The forest consultation places two forms of belonging in conflict. Yunyun wants to protect her new town friends, but an actual rare summons binds the future chief to village/national duty (P0449–0493). Megumin first mistakes the private request for rivalry, then objects through action to reserve status. The need to show the letter to other recipients is voiced after its first paper-plane discard P0476, but before its destruction P0498–0501. Kazuma’s catch/mana support follows the cast, not a shown prior joint plan (P0504–0505). Megumin offers a practical excuse for Yunyun’s desired stay while advertising her own specialist value; Yunyun’s visible pleased reaction supports benefit, but the precise cause at P0511 is narrator inference and the reserve-pride accusation receives a flinch rather than a full confession (P0506–0520).

### 3.6. Final chapter — the shortcut fails because the friend accepts it

A fake little-sister wake-up briefly works on Kazuma’s tastes, but still ends with thrown bedding and a qualified compliment (FP0006–0027). Asked to solve everything like a blue tanuki, Aqua states bounded powers and turns sealing evil into a threat to his lower body (P0029–0039). His alternative is conditional waiting for other forces, then seeking safer guarded barrier work; neither Iris’s victory nor the king’s defeat is shown (P0041–0048). Aqua identifies another apparent route through the friendly officer. Her rush to Wiz meets an absent owner and Vanir’s actual limitation against strong beings, not universal clairvoyance (P0049–0078).

The weather-charm sale distinguishes raw magic from context-sensitive value. Wiz has checked side effects but not the relation between seasonal restriction and local rain; Vanir’s twenty-year weather account is his report. Repeated refusal falters into a request for details when the bundle adds a secret erotic pillow; no completed purchase is shown (P0081–0114). Divine/undead confrontation begins while Aqua accepts a cookie. Vanir interposes and Wiz readily consents; that consent produces Aqua’s incongruous life-value rebuke before deeper gratitude and love make her unable to continue (P0119–0168). Vanir calls Wiz by name and invokes their contract, then actually steps aside after her choice; respect for her intention is Kazuma’s supported but still interpretive explanation P0151. No purification is cast. A friend’s chosen trust changes the agent’s appraisal and moral willingness, not her demonstrated raw ability.

Kazuma returns, obtains food and carries it to Aqua. His questions about losing Wiz also concern losing the party, but he is uncertain whether heavenly return permits later visits (P0172–0202). His food-based confidence that she will come downstairs has failed by nightfall P0208. This does not mean she stays shut away all night: the later balcony appearance is shown P0259–0264. Alone, he is embarrassed by the wish not to be separated, momentarily imagines an appealing quiet three-person household, then admits boredom without her (P0212–0244). This counterfactual ambivalence is real; it is not a chosen durable reorganization. Stealth protects the subsequent approach from an unwanted sexual interpretation (P0245–0257).

Moonlit beauty is privately recognized, never delivered as the straightforward compliment he withholds (P0259–0287). Their talk retains age insults and sealing threats but permits her own long heavenly history, boredom and hardship to matter. Her claim not to mind the town’s rejection coexists with his report of earlier distress (P0288–0305). The quiet 「帰りたいなぁ」 is expressly not a demand on him (P0307–0314). His recognition of unknown earlier friends/life and forced transport responsibility precedes the limited constructive offer (P0315–0322). Mines, teleport routes and repeated Explosion are proposals, not completed preparations or an accepted guarantee to fight personally. Aqua’s amused reaction includes a real bodily-destruction/resurrection limit, while another seal threat ends his retaliatory insult in apology (P0324–0330).

Her later full-name question asks whether being sent to this world was good and whether he regrets it (P0342). He privately weighs his current life and answers without regret (P0345–0351). Her relief is observed; reciprocal guilt is his tentative interpretation P0354. Vanity and blunt refusal return in the goodnight warning, yet he returns to bed considering means, notices that he is seriously planning, then rejects the foolish thought and closes his eyes before actually falling asleep P0369–0370, with the final wish for strength framed in drowsiness (P0355–0373). The sincerity has changed what he is willing to think about; it has not removed every defensive habit or completed an operation.

### 3.7. Epilogue — leaving and asking to be found

Kazuma is awake before he embraces Darkness and performs sleep; she initially objects, then stops resisting. Megumin accuses the wrong initiator, and he adds a false assault claim before learning it is noon (EpP0003–0020). Neither the later blush nor silence erases that chronology. Aqua’s departure letter then starts with embarrassing allegations about everyone’s ordinary habits, including laundry behavior not independently shown here. The narrator crumples it before Megumin retrieves it (P0023–0034). Its billion followers are hyperbole; Darkness’s few-hundred correction is her estimate, not an audited total (P0035–0043).

The declared Demon King destination produces actual standing/jumping and immediate pursuit intention (P0045–0054). The source does not yet show a completed departure by the pursuers, route, encounter or exact escort. Aqua’s savings are reported; hiring skilled adventurers is Darkness’s hypothesis P0052. The erased postscript reads 「探してください」, reversing the expected do-not-look formula (P0059–0065). Kazuma infers embarrassed erasure rather than observing the act of writing. The novel thus ends on a departure that asserts a goal and asks for contact at once. It does not settle the goddess’s destination, the companion arrangement, the city’s defense, Serena’s royal transfer or any later-volume resolution.

## 4. Major character findings

### 4.1. Kazuma — caring does not make the method benevolent

The opening bargain is a credible selection from the options he actually notices. He knows he is outmatched, fears exposing Aqua and expects his companions to turn discovery into confrontation. Accepting secrecy reduces immediate danger and preserves comfort. His private account credits their asymmetric contributions rather than believing every public legend about his personal might (C1P0143–0153/P0199–0209). That realism coexists with vanity: he welcomes recognition, volunteers vices during a moral speech and narrates a visibly shaken refusal as steel will (P0073–0076/P0101–0126). A simulator should preserve both the useful practical self-assessment and the compensating story, including their order.

Aqua’s exclusion changes the value of doing nothing. The evidence is not merely that another person looks sad: he notices a deviation from her familiar self-caused trouble, feels repeated discomfort, cannot dismiss it by denying sexual attraction and finally receives an explicit belonging question (P0350–0385/P0459–0468). Meanwhile he has already considered and rejected unsafe cures, binds and outside consultation. The ensuing deployment is thus a costly action selected from a narrowed perceived option set, with self-preservation, affection, resentment and civic attachment all active. He uses the granted solo-opposition exception, but practical help from companions remains indispensable. Later denials that he acted for Aqua conflict with his own prior attention and his unguarded revenge admission; they do not delete it (C2P0715–0723; C3P0456).

The campaign is clever about objects and often cruel about people. Rounded arrows, range checks, large-target wire, stealth, mana purchases, delayed police handling and counter-debt inference show planning. Unknown coffee temperature, reliance on already-embarrassed guarantors, thanking the person whose control route he had nearly identified show bounded attention and failed integration of relevant knowledge (C2P0177–0214/P0330–0356/P0373–0416/P0745–0767). The rescuers’ initial one-hand restraint also fails despite Serena’s warning; Aqua preserves the bottle and the group changes the application strategy (C3P0416–0443). Local skill does not imply consistently good risk calibration. Nor does effectiveness make his romantic advice, public exposure or coerced bodily payments consensual. The refusal-as-desire rhetoric and deliberate misdescription of his own acts are directly available evidence of social manipulation.

Domination changes his agency without removing all initiative. The narrative gives cognitive/motor impairment, imposed abandonment of restraints and a changed allegiance/address state (C2P0757–0815/P0824–0834). Within that state he computes costs, withholds information, resists unwanted commands through pain, exploits priestly doctrine and pressures the captor. No source establishes a secret conscious resistance plan, and the internal narrator approves of staying altered until the cure actually takes effect. Conversely, an explanation that makes every act an externally specified order would erase his own newly elaborated demands and choices. The smallest supported model is altered loyalty and inhibited moral restraint with substantial surviving inference/appetite, not either unrestricted normal agency or an empty puppet.

Post-cure refusal to murder is partly fear and partly attachment; the text makes both visible rather than forcing virtue or cowardice as the whole cause (C3P0480–0497). The reset is a deliberately unequal mutual cost whose affordability he considers: lost levels matter, but money and his low ceiling make his rebuilding easier (P0505–0514/P0532). His solo-completion claim is a perspective claim, not the event inventory. The subsequent death and willingness to abandon revival expose how real repeated danger remains beneath the boasts (C4P0023–0041/P0053–0088). Later, the wish for cheat power emerges from a changed concern, not the acquisition of that power (FP0359–0373).

His developing acceptance of Aqua’s separate life is the strongest local relational change. At first, her wish to leave feels like a slight; he imagines both thoughtful and heedless motives (C4P0279–0296). Food delivery maintains contact before he can ask directly whether their shared life matters to her (FP0172–0244). Her undemanding wish later makes his own lack of knowledge about her former world salient, and responsibility for having forced her here changes the question from whether she should want to stay to whether he should help her return (FP0307–0322). Sincere no-regret self-disclosure and later pursuit intention coexist with sexual teasing, annoyance and uncertainty. None is evidence that insults will now disappear or that every vulnerability produces restraint: actual tears, continued torment and pleasure remain adverse to any broad tears-always-stop-him rule (C3 diary and street episodes; C4P0425–0441).

### 4.2. Aqua — instrumental power, social need and chosen moral limits

The volume repeatedly demonstrates why one competence label fails. Purification works on seasoning and potion stock, but the result can defeat the practical goal. Resurrection remains the party’s real contingency and is reported as actual work after Kazuma’s death. In contrast, the serious patient’s rejected treatment is an access failure, not shown failed execution; his request recruits willingness before anyone blocks her (PrP0008–0019; C1P0308–0346; C2P0675–0677; C4P0017). Her attempted cure later has a genuine first failure and adaptive coordinated retry (C3P0416–0443). Capacity, readiness, task information, permission/access and completed outcome need separate entries.

Her early hurt is tied to useful recognition in a known community. The denied patient and former drinking companions leave her unusually withdrawn, and she asks whether the town needs its goddess (C1P0365–0380/P0465–0468). This does not prove that every act is a search for worship or that ordinary service is insincere. Her actual rush to the patient, hydration attempt for Wiz and cure logistics show concrete helping. Vanity and information failure can distort those acts without eliminating their purpose. Nor does Kazuma’s need for resurrection exhaust her relational value: his later boredom/absence thoughts identify more than a spare healing resource.

The heavenly message renews the urgency of her long-established return goal and gives it a concrete institutional rationale. An institution values her effective recruiting again, while ordinary divinity does not confer unlimited power to defeat the king. She admits limits, develops a partly useful barrier/alliance idea and notices the projected opening when defenders leave the castle (C4P0176–0230/P0393–0409; FP0035). Her assassination fallback is reckless and her pet-potion diversion is unconsulted resource use; these do not cancel the specific strategic insight. The projected castle situation is still unknown. No actual new barrier feat, enemy deployment or successful dragon solution is shown.

The long persuasion campaign tests different social routes: wake-up coercion, chosen-hero status, false moral reform, attempted sleep persuasion, curated survey and sister fantasy. Failure to secure agreement prompts escalation and tactical variation rather than proof that the goal was never serious (C4P0144–0262/P0298–0318/P0337–0350; FP0006–0060). The stone exchange is actual harmful pressure on a companion and property, answered with a planned muddy counter that leaves her crying. Bystander enjoyment does not establish mutual consent (C4P0425–0444). The behavior remains part of her agency, alongside being wronged by exclusion.

Wiz’s answer changes the selected goal rather than merely defeating an ability. Aqua expects opposition to cleansing an undead officer, accepts hospitality during the announcement and is unsettled when the friend consents (FP0119–0140). Sustained trust, gratitude and love make the requested act difficult to carry through; she recoils and leaves without casting (P0140–0168). The narrator labels conscience P0168, and the observers’ gaze P0163–0166 is also present, so the scene does not isolate a uniquely private altruistic cause. There is no new power failure and no need to invent a more competent alternative purifier. Her later lost divine confidence is partly about not fulfilling the impersonal role she proclaimed, while attachment has constrained her willing action. The quiet return wish and the final request to be found preserve both autonomous desire and relational need. Actual solitude, hired escort, travel route and completion remain unknown at EpP0065.

### 4.3. Megumin — assertive attachment and specialization have costs

Her investigation is genuinely informative: no reward claim implies a possible card-disclosure problem, a hypothesis later supported by the dark-priest employment limitation (C1P0176; C3P0067–0068). Her trust in Kazuma initially operates under missing facts; support is not informed assent to the enemy pact. When he chooses confrontation, she enjoys daring and praises him, while Darkness independently objects (C1P0390/P0495–0500). Their different evaluations are actual ensemble variation.

Reclaiming the altered man combines chosen intimacy, rivalry and care. She distinguishes old hesitant excuse-making from new shamelessness, voluntarily displays what she wants him to see and is pleased to claim him when others hesitate (C3P0319/P0370–0405). Her actual hold makes escape harder, but one hand is not enough: the cure still gets stolen and thrown. The later hug is a different restraint configuration under explicit teammate instruction, and she complains at renewed cheek rubbing before the pour (P0412–0443). After restoration she ends the hug despite his healing pretext (P0464–0466). Consent is event- and person-specific, not a general permission inferred from intimacy.

The defense summons independently threatens her standing as a specialist. The document supplies reserve status after her eager readiness; Yunyun hedgedly infers that lacking advanced magic is probably why (C4P0467–0474). Her first response is physical disposal of the letter; after learning other recipients need it, she later destroys its area with Explosion and identifies herself as first-team material (P0475–0506). This is technical success with administrative cost. The reserve/advanced-magic judgment clearly threatens her standing; whether it explicitly denigrates, replaces or imitates Explosion must be assessed separately under the frozen M01 trigger. No named-rival-spell or formal-debate condition is added. Her magic remains technically powerful without requiring the organization’s practical classification to be wrong in every respect.

Yunyun’s actual wish to protect town friends supplies a simultaneous relational stake. Megumin’s forceful excuse-making permits that desired path, and Yunyun visibly receives it with mixed pleasure (P0483–0512). Yet Kazuma’s accusation of reserve resentment and her flinch retain a competing cause (P0517–0520). The sequence cannot be purified into either altruism or spite. Her own intervention violates a known shared-document requirement, and Kazuma’s mana support occurs afterward. A simulation should preserve her ability to aid a timid friend through a forceful solution while charging the externalities and identity payoff to the same act.

### 4.4. Darkness — trust, public responsibility and dignity do not make one rule

Darkness gathers information, protects an endangered companion and has institutional resources the group uses. She also arrives expecting a date, holds an embarrassing hope under denial, and can be manipulated through public reputation (C1P0174/P0185–0232/P0257–0264). Dress and public face-hiding independently establish those local stakes; no reply should be invented where the source supplies only a bodily response.

Her release intervention conflicts with the very impartiality both men expect of her (C2P0024/P0046–0047/P0064–0085). She interprets a trusted companion’s silence as a burden he cannot disclose and offers aftermath responsibility, while the lack of a complaint qualifies the actual legal situation. This is a consequential shift toward trusted-person discretion, not proof of universal corruption or a stable willingness to excuse everything. Kazuma’s disclosure then imposes a dignity cost, and her later refusal to act as guarantor is a meaningful limit that is actually reported as applied (P0087–0115/P0373). His reading of mild anger cannot erase the expressed boundary.

Her immediate interposition before Death protects Aqua without complete knowledge of the enemy mechanics (C3P0343–0351). Reflected injury then exposes the limit to force. Her later willingness to kill a dangerous civic threat is grounded in an actual public duty, but the captive’s own harm and Darkness’s pain-seeking interrogation coexist (P0498–0528). She does not become a different person whenever noble duty is active. Newly learned feedback can recruit a personally gratifying use of the same mechanism rather than purely task-oriented restraint.

Aqua’s flirting accusation and the bedroom’s false seduction/assault claims produce specific personal objections (C4P0349–0350; EpP0008–0018). The latter sequence begins with Kazuma’s conscious initiation, includes her initial protest and then stopped resistance. That chronology must survive both comic embarrassment and speculation about romantic appetite. No new explicit Kazuma declaration of love or negotiated reciprocal relationship is established by these scenes. Darkness’s observable affection and hope are not evidence of every belief he has about them.

### 4.5. Yunyun — valued inclusion recruits participation under persistent moral/social anxiety

The failed dinner-companion consultation is not a lack of magic. She later asks whether the covert task is criminal, then accepts emphatically when a trusted person uniquely relies on her and actually casts invisibility support (C1P0218–0223; C2P0217–0236). Silent is explicitly part of the spoken next task P0236; its exact completed cast is not separately staged there. Secrecy painfully recalls her lack of confidants, but she does not withdraw from the shown support. Moral concern and the pleasure of inclusion coexist, rather than one disproving the other.

The forest dilemma supplies a different constraint: town friendships are now real enough to motivate defense, while next-chief duty makes a rare summons difficult to disregard (C4P0483–0493). Megumin’s force helps her articulate the wish and later provides an excuse, though the precise reason for her happy expression is partly Kazuma’s interpretation. Repeated labels that she is lonely should not erase the new contacts or their practical importance. Conversely, calling them friends does not certify Dust or the masked shop worker as harmless company. Her vulnerability is directly her own; it is not automatically evidence that Megumin has entered the frozen own-vulnerable-appeal role.

### 4.6. Serena — an effective specialist can lose the social problem created by her own order

The infiltrator is not a generic stupid antagonist. She gives risk-avoiders convenient stories, stages a recognition-building miracle, offers useful ordinary advice when no magic is needed, exploits gratitude/debt, learns from harassment and uses a relaxed truce as a real opening (C1P0034–0043; C2P0141–0145/P0268–0312/P0745–0815). She can also abandon calculated public restraint under anger and underestimate the costs of a servant whose obedience is not passivity. Her exact rule explanations remain interested accounts until supported by observed transitions.

The self-serving master/servant account becomes structurally unstable: freeing his allegiance is unacceptable to him, harming a sincere co-believer rebounds, and each command can be recast as another debt. Her reported card constraint limits the adventurer-request/guild-reward route while her devotee consumes resources, and the private diary confirms that victim distress is often what the narrator finds satisfying. The hostile outcomes do not establish an invisible greater plan by either party. Even when desperate, she retains lethal capacity once faith becomes exclusive again and acts before restraint reaches her (C3P0538–0548). Real danger survives extended humiliation.

### 4.7. Wiz, Vanir and Eris — supporting figures expose different care mechanisms

Wiz’s technical power is distinct from purchasing judgment. A season-limited high-magic charm can be real yet locally unsalable, and she has checked the wrong constraint after prior reprimands (FP0081–0114). Her consent to purification is a much more coherent value choice: she describes achieved home/friend goals, recognizes another person’s desired return and chooses a trusted purifier (P0140–0160). This is neither consent to every past punishment nor proof the act is completed. The substitute lich is an offer only; closed narrative cannot identify or deliver that person.

Vanir anticipates profitable traffic and pressures both customers, but actual constraints and unexpected Aqua behavior remain. He is stunned when inventory is purified and explicitly states difficulty seeing powerful targets (C2P0675–0685; FP0074). The contract/name protest and physical shielding show a relation that matters beyond insulting a bad buyer; his later withdrawal is observed, while respect for Wiz’s choice is narrated inference (FP0125–0151). His new fee in the diary remains a report, not a completed adjudication of whether it fits an earlier promise.

Eris supplies genuine reassurance, accounts of the party’s work and urgent institutional information. Her teasing reception and explicit denials are not evidence of secret romantic interference. The world-risk message competes with mediated body panic and is later reconstructed (C4P0006–0122/P0176–0188). A goddess trying to make herself heard is not automatically a fresh case of taking on excessive sincere service requests; the exact incoming-burden antecedent would still need evidence.

## 5. Relationships and ensemble scripts

The party’s default abuse is a shared interaction history, not blanket permission. V15 repeatedly shows a companion correctly predicting another’s tactics while objecting to the immediate act. Aqua and Kazuma anticipate first-climber betrayal; Megumin expects covert sexual excuses; Darkness’s first arrest intervention is followed by an actual limit after public exposure. Familiarity improves prediction and can deepen hurt at once. It cannot substitute for consent, all relevant information or a universal moral reset after a punchline.

| Relationship | Trigger and local appraisal | Observed interaction and consequence | Boundary for later simulation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Kazuma–Aqua: rejected useful member | Former patients/friends refuse her, unlike her usual self-caused trouble; he notices a changed familiar person (C1P0350–0385). | Quietly accumulated unease, rejected unsafe alternatives, then planned self-injuring retaliation and resource/handoff requests (P0395–0486). | Affection and instrumental resurrection value coexist. Earlier care makes a newly care-free baseline false; the specific behavioral commitment is later. |
| Kazuma–Aqua: departure and home | Her desired heaven appears to devalue their year, then he recognizes a prior life he does not know (C4P0279–0296; FP0307–0322). | Dinner delivery, insomnia, incomplete/indirect questions, limited constructive offer, honest no-regret reply, later pursuit intention. | Home matters beyond utility without requiring every efficient separation to be rejected. No completed new stable living arrangement or search is shown. |
| Kazuma–Aqua: everyday adversaries | Arrest rivalry, insults, petty resource conflicts and stone coercion recruit reciprocal escalation (C2P0475–0487; C4P0414–0444). | He wishes to protect her yet considers tripping her in the chase; later mud produces real tears and satisfaction. | Preserve unexecuted thoughts separately from acts. Protectiveness does not automatically stop harshness once care is established. |
| Kazuma–Darkness: trust and discretion | He cannot explain a threat; she reads silence in the context of a trusted companion, despite usual public fairness (C2P0064–0079). | Actual release help and unlocking are followed by his grateful cling. | Relationship-specific trust can change institutional choice; no complaint exists, and arbitrary illegality is not established solely by favor. |
| Kazuma–Darkness: public dignity | An idealizing officer dismisses the possibility of romance, and he retaliates with true private details (C2P0087–0107). | Shame, explicit objection and later refusal to guarantee him again (P0110/P0373). | Literal truth can be deliberate humiliation. Later mild mood and continuing affection do not erase a specific boundary or guarantee unlimited future help. |
| Kazuma–Megumin: reclaiming and contact | Altered shamelessness threatens the familiar relationship; she distinguishes it from his old hesitant strategies (C3P0319). | Voluntary display, claiming him, holding/hugging during cure; after restoration she actively ends his prolonged healing-pretext hug. | Her chosen exposure is specific to her, person, moment and purpose. Actual restraint and affection coexist; first approach still fails. |
| Megumin–Darkness: different ways to support | Kazuma's daring charge and later desire to reclaim him activate different valuations (C1P0493–0500; C3P0366–0380). | Specialist excitement meets legal objection; possessive acceptance meets initial hesitation and alarm. | Neither woman is the permanent moral objector or permanent sexual rival. Do not turn contextually attributable P0367 into independently tagged speech. |
| Megumin–Yunyun: rivalry as forceful aid | A private summons is mistaken for challenge; later reserve status and Yunyun's conflicting defense duty become simultaneous stakes (C4P0449–0493). | Threatened duel turns to consultation; destruction of a needed letter enables an excuse but costs shared administration and serves pride. | Friendship benefit does not cancel identity threat or known cost. Source confirms wanted town defense but not the exact private mix behind Megumin's act. |
| Kazuma–Yunyun: relied-on specialist | His unique-reliance appeal follows her criminality question (C2P0217–0236). | Eager acceptance and actual invisibility support; secrecy then touches her loneliness. | An objection is compatible with participation. No assurance that a vaguely righteous framing cures moral uncertainty, and no invented unshown cast. |
| Kazuma–Serena: calculated pact to actual domination | Each recognizes practical self-interest; apparent settlement lowers vigilance and thanks becomes a control opening (C1P0127–0138; C2P0745–0815). | Secret safety bargain, harassment, observation, cognitive capture, altered devotion and escalating burdens on the captor. | The hostile operation's collapse cannot retroactively prove the captive planned it. Coercion and surviving self-directed tactics must both remain. |
| Kazuma–Serena: partial attachment after harm | Cohabitation leaves affective residue despite manipulation and cruelty (C3P0480–0497). | He rejects murder but chooses a costly nonlethal reset; she still attempts lethal escape. | Affection is neither mutual safety nor pardon. His refusal to kill is explicit and cannot be replaced by a purely instrumental account. |
| Aqua–Wiz: natural enemies as chosen friends | Goddess/lich roles make extermination available; Wiz's own consent and trusted love make it morally costly (FP0119–0168). | Cookies, a reversed life-value admonition, sustained gratitude, retreat without casting and later withdrawal. | A voluntary stop differs from insufficient power or interrupted access. Wiz's consent here cannot authorize unrelated punishments elsewhere. |
| Wiz–Vanir: insulting commerce and actual shared project | Bad purchases irritate him; possible final departure threatens their dungeon agreement and companionship (FP0081–0151). | Merchandising coercion coexists with shielding, direct name use, contractual protest and eventual withdrawal. | The observed step aside is firmer evidence than Kazuma's exact respect explanation. No completed substitute handoff or abandoned project is shown. |
| Aqua–Vanir: identity rivalry with collateral harm | Ruined stock and direct deity/devil challenges turn commerce into a fight (C2P0689–0713). | Beams/Reflect, harmed Wiz, address imitation and an identity complaint, then sugar-water repair discussion. | The priest and devil can fail the bystander while arguing over status. Written imitation is source evidence; no acoustic reproduction is available. |
| Kazuma–Eris: relief and renewed obligation | Death demoralization receives familiarity and eye-level care, while bodily panic competes with world information (C4P0023–0122). | Proposal/jealousy teasing, refusal to revive, failed happy-memory prompt, hurried return and retrospective message. | Denied jealousy is not a secret motive. Apparent peace is his experience, not proof she has no duty or time pressure. |
| Party–institutions: reciprocal dependence | Police, noble guarantees, card/reward rules and village defense mobilization constrain choices. | Release requests, threats, responsibility refusal, actual captive/intelligence handoff and a destroyed shared summons. | Institutional competence is neither uniform corruption nor an irrelevant backdrop. Record authority, knowledge and local legal conditions separately. |

Knowledge distribution is part of every relationship. The party trusts Kazuma without knowing the pact. Aqua does not know the gifted potion's function when she guards it. Kazuma does not know whether former enemy colleagues would take his side. Darkness does not initially know reflected-harm mechanics. Yunyun knows her own wish better than the witness who clarifies it, and confirms his interpretation. Wiz may consent to purification without having consented to secret merchandise. Even after restoring the captive, no one acquires perfect access to his previous controlled inner state. These mismatches generate humor, tactical failure and genuine vulnerability through the same ensemble structure.

The final letter preserves the double movement of the bond. A declared goal and actual absence make the departure consequential; the erased request for pursuit preserves a desire for contact. Kazuma's immediate pursuit intention matters because the previous scene left him planning from bed, but the source stops before the search is performed. A forward model should allow an agent to want both a separate goal and to be found by familiar people, without reducing either motive to insincerity.

## 6. Decisions, errors and asymmetric competence

An outcome can be technically successful, strategically harmful and relationally meaningful at the same time. The following cases preserve the options noticed and the actual result; they do not rank global intelligence or silently promote reported plans into completed feats.

| Case and locator | Stimulus → appraisal → noticed options → choice | Actual result and smallest supported causal account | Competence/choice boundary |
|---|---|---|---|
| Soy sauce, PrP0008–0019 | Accidental contact threatens seasoning and face; deny, seek indulgence, then comply with the eating test. | Purified water tastes unsatisfactory; request salt and promise replacement. | Strong domain effect, poor ordinary material control and defensive concession; no technical failure to purify. |
| Safety pact, C1P0127–0153 | Outmatched by an exposed officer, Kazuma notices solo-fight risk, companions' potential escalation and a secrecy bargain. | Accepts safety, supplies loan and retains uneasy surveillance. | Locally coherent risk avoidance; civic language partly exculpatory. Neither pure heroism nor no concern for others. |
| Hidden investigation, C1P0174–0180 | Darkness's contacts and Megumin's reward/card inference expose independent anomalies. | Useful information is obtained, then Kazuma forbids continued inquiry because he knows more. | Their actual competence survives the narrator's habitually-bizarre framing. Cooperation is limited by asymmetric information. |
| Patient access, C1P0303–0346 | Genuine injury and named request recruit Aqua's offer and approach; crowd denies access, recipient later refuses. | No Aqua healing is shown; Serena improves but does not fully close wounds. | Capacity/readiness/opportunity/result separate. Denied service cannot establish an execution failure. |
| Deliberated retaliation, C1P0395–0505 | Vulnerable companion and restricted solo exception compete with danger; reject uncertain curse removal/bind/outside help, then choose a prepared kick. | Buffs and strike actual; subsequent injury/legal aftermath shown next chapter. | Practical anticipation includes deliberately accepting self-injury and police custody. It remains assault and uses party labor despite solitary framing. |
| Release and humiliation, C2P0064–0115 | Trusted silence recruits Darkness's discretion; officer's class disdain then recruits Kazuma's retaliatory truth inventory. | Actual help, public shame and a future guarantor refusal. | Social perception can identify an effective wound without benevolent judgment; short-term victory creates a later resource cost. |
| Nice-person counseling, C2P0135–0173 | Useful generic advice is treated as a commitment to date; refusal becomes alleged hidden desire. | Imposed handholding and a fraying public mask, not shown romantic success. | Deliberate trolling/manipulation, not a factual inability to read ordinary refusal. Victim wrongdoing does not consent to this act. |
| Cup shot, C2P0177–0214 | Direct harm rebounds; target the cup with nonlethal preparation to test indirect causation. | Accuracy succeeds, guessed temperature fails, reflected scald teaches that indirect damage counts. | Planning/precision coexist with a knowledge gap. Later hypothesized explosive-potion attack is not attempted. |
| Yunyun recruitment/ice, C2P0217–0260 | Kazuma needs stealth; unique reliance recruits a lonely but morally hesitant specialist, mana stones permit prolonged ice work. | Invisibility support is shown; windowless hut becomes a toilet trap after repeated overnight work. | Cooperative domain deployment, substantial resource/effort costs and a cruel selected goal. Silent is a stated next spell, not separately staged as completed. |
| Robe bind, C2P0315–0356 | Prayer pose removes hands from skirt; ordinary bind may be limited, so use special large-target wire and cloth geometry. | Capture/exposure holds; inability to self-release is his observed inference; public cry increases humiliation. | Adaptable use of mundane geometry and magic; force avoidance does not remove coercive sexual harm. |
| Debt hypothesis/capture, C2P0384–0416/P0602–0638/P0745–0767 | Kazuma compares healing, thanks, unmet help and momentary release; apparent settlement lowers vigilance. | Stronger causal hypothesis followed by personally expressing thanks and actual impairment. | Inference need not imply reliable self-monitoring or consolidated guard behavior. Contextual relaxation is visible; exact rule still attributed before later explanation. |
| Hydrating Wiz, C2P0675–0685 | Visible fading and the claim of useless potions make water conversion look helpful. | Purification succeeds; cure inventory is lost and holy residue stings Wiz. | Task information and goal alignment fail, while domain power executes. No known preservation assignment is invented. |
| Controlled servant, C2P0813–0958; C3P0006–0118 | Coercive order removes restraints; loyalty and preserving control make payment/refusal/information into debt problems. | Priest loses resources/other puppets, suffers sexual coercion, cannot cheaply dismiss a sincere believer. | Significant surviving computation under changed allegiance; favorable strategic consequences do not prove an intended counterplan or unchanged free baseline. |
| Grave work, C3P0258–0286 | Disgust, belief pretext and avoiding repayment oppose a task; capacity allocation prevents a new corpse puppet. | Painful refusal, withheld timing advice, failed spell and failed control reallocation. | No evidence of a universal priest/mage incapacity. Kazuma's point label is invented; Serena's doctrinal/control account and actual failure have distinct access. |
| Cure logistics, C3P0416–0443 | Known resistant target, one-hand hold and confidence; Steal/throw feedback threatens the only bottle. | Aqua saves it, Serena commands stillness, Megumin changes hold and Aqua pours successfully. | First failure plus local adaptation, resource preservation and cooperation; no flawless or solo success claim. |
| Reset, C3P0480–0548; C4P0156–0165 | Killing is rejected; freeing remains unsafe; matched reset imposes an affordable relative cost. | Choice is interrupted by Death, then later revival/reset/handoff actually completed. | Willing sacrifice and opportunistic asymmetry coexist. Declared solo completion does not erase teammates; future attack cancellation/bounty remain inferred or reported promises. |
| Return persuasion, C4P0144–0318 | Heaven needs Aqua; direct appeals fail, so she tests hero status, duty language and sleep whispers. | Kazuma resists; hypocrisy is exposed, attempted brainwashing confessed. | Changing tactics and goal persistence, not proof of successful mind-control magic or an actual sexual visit. |
| Survey, C4P0337–0350 | Public suffering would justify the mission; actual responses are unhelpful, so select a different sheet. | Wrong sheet reveals curation; both surveys contain interested/absurd claims. | Motivated presentation rather than no data collection ability. Anonymous role labels do not independently identify every respondent. |
| Pet potion/counter, C4P0410–0444 | Aqua treats future dragon growth as mission investment; Kazuma had a different resource plan. | Rooster grows, scarce potion is gone, stone pressure meets adaptive wind/water/earth and real tears. | Goal conflict and unilateral spending; water's ineffectiveness leads to a different selected material. Ordinary laundry cost falls to Darkness. |
| Shared summons, C4P0467–0520 | Reserve status threatens Megumin's identity while Yunyun wants to stay for town defense. | First disposal, known document need, actual destruction, later mana support, useful excuse and wounded-pride evidence. | Technical execution succeeds; practical externality was known before the later act. Supported friend benefit does not erase status motive. |
| Weather charm, FP0081–0114 | Wiz checks visible magic and side effects, assumes usable-now means useful-now. | Power is plausibly real but seasonal demand is nearly absent by Vanir's report; a sexual bonus finally elicits interest in the details, with no completed purchase shown. | Wrong constraint selected, not no magical evaluation skill. No actual weather-change experiment is performed. |
| Requested purification, FP0119–0168 | Aqua's impersonal route home meets trusted voluntary acceptance and explicit love. | She physically retreats and runs without casting. | Chosen moral/relational stop; not demonstrated inability, crowd interruption or completed cleansing. |
| Door care/nightfall, FP0172–0208 | Kazuma chooses food delivery and relational questioning, expects hunger to restore routine. | Food is requested outside; Aqua has not emerged by nightfall, later appears on balcony. | Practical contact succeeds; the casual timing prediction fails. Do not extend 夜になっても to all-night confinement. |
| Balcony offer/letter, FP0307–0373; EpP0045–0065 | Quiet home wish recruits responsibility; later declared risky departure recruits standing/jumping and pursuit intention. | Limited future proposals and honest disclosure, then immediate intent to follow; no actual search result. | Motivation changes before ability does. Actual companions/escort are unknown, and the intended next act is not a completed journey. |

Across cases, the narrator often knows a sensible alternative and still chooses a costlier option for status, appetite, resentment or a valued bond. In other cases he lacks a fact, predicts badly or cannot execute safely. The distinction matters: learning from reflected coffee supports a local causal update, while magic theory does not immunize him against gratitude, ordinary familiarity does not prevent a failed cure approach, and a correct practical counter can be chosen to continue hurting someone. Successful improvisation, moral restraint and general reliability cannot be substituted for one another.

The competent contributions are distributed. Darkness supplies information, authority, shielding, capture and care; Megumin supplies inference, intimacy/restraint, binding and a powerful but costly intervention; Aqua supplies actual buffs, bottle rescue, purification, cure deployment and resurrection; Yunyun supplies a specific stealth capability; Vanir supplies information/items; Eris supplies afterlife/institutional access. Claims that Kazuma acted alone are analytically important narrator statements, but the actual result depends on this network. The ten separate append payloads must retain those different task contributions rather than credit only the last visible actor.

## 7. Humor mechanisms, literalness and complete-census scope

The central comic engine is reciprocal constraint. Kazuma first tries to preserve safety by making a private deal, then repeatedly turns the enemy’s debt, reflection and control rules against her. Once the reversal works too well, the nominal master becomes a desperate provider and the nominal puppet makes himself expensive to command. The reversal is literary evidence about selected appraisals and choices; it does not make manipulation, pain or an expressed refusal unreal. The diary makes this particularly visible: it presents his altered valuation, documents actual coercive bargains and contains a separately marked invented erotic passage. The invented passage at C3P0029 is neither enacted contact nor Serena’s actual speech.

| Comic process / primary location | Setup, response and causal work | Literalness and model limit |
|---|---|---|
| Refuted household innocence, PrP0007–0018 | Aqua’s repeated denial encounters the practical challenge to use the seasoning; concession appears as a request for salt. The retort exposes accountability through a familiar meal. | Actual handling/taste disagreement; no medical service adoption merely because water/purification occurs. |
| Pet hierarchy turned on the household, PrP0019–0044 | The apparently subordinate creature attacks the claimed master; a peace interpretation ends with Aqua calling for help. | Actions and help call are real within the narrated scene. Species, observed fire and realistic damage require separate authority; Kazuma does not watch the fire instant. |
| Legendary villain versus precarious visitor, C1P0012–0153 | Rough speech yields courtesy, a reusable maiden story becomes confessed fabrication, military recruitment ends in a loan, and Kazuma punctures his own hero legend by crediting actual contributors. | New disclosures remain Serena’s claims; earlier readers did not already know the mechanism. The loan has later causal use. |
| Noble/legal frame punctured by household intimacy, C1P0185–0232; C2P0054–0113 | Darkness’s practical institutional help is accompanied by appearance planning and later public exposure of private history. The officer’s idealized image and Kazuma’s account collide. | Real legal labor and unwanted embarrassment coexist. Kazuma’s claim she is not angry is an interpretation, not consent or an acquittal. |
| Relationship wish no professional can provide, C1P0210–0223; C2P0217–0241 | The supposed universal consultant fails on the mundane shared-meal wish; later recruitment succeeds too quickly when Kazuma says only Yunyun can help. | The eventual role is real; loneliness cannot prove she endorses each tactic or that every proposed spell was cast. |
| Borrowed piety and public exposure, C2P0315–0375 | A praying pose becomes a mechanical robe-and-wire opportunity. Followers regain momentary clarity yet gather to hide her; a new crowd worsens the exposure and legal consequences. | Coercive stripping and her stop requests are actual. The custom Bind’s duration and comic crowd magnitude do not establish harmlessness or a general consent rule. |
| Specialist marketing defeats itself, C2P0589–0712 | Vanir prices the enemy’s entire purse, offers liberation goods, then Aqua converts stock into water to help Wiz. Enemies briefly laugh together before an insult recruits a separate fight. | Actual domain success destroys useful stock and holy water stings Wiz; later she is seen scorched during the fight, with the precise collateral route inferred by Kazuma. Product destruction precedes the divine insult and cannot be attributed backward to it. |
| The obedient puppet becomes the master’s burden, C2P0834–0958; C3P0006–0118 | Logical loyalty, debt preservation, commanded restraint removal and expensive compensation make Serena fund and endure the person she wanted as a tool. | Genuine altered allegiance coexists with discretionary exploitation. Tears, food deprivation, unwanted touching and threats are load-bearing limits, not erased by the reversed role. |
| Sacred certainty versus eclectic practice, C3P0131–0186 | Serena asks about exclusive faith; Kazuma describes mixed seasonal practice and innumerable gods, then offends and flatters her in adjacent turns. | Native ruby identifies 八百万 as やおよろず, “myriad”; no exact eight-million census. 無宗派 and 無宗教 retain their distinct lexical roles. |
| Explicit performance cue that fails as an instruction, C3P0271–0286 | A nonexistent resource name seems to explain failed control; Serena recognizes the real capacity problem, while repeated “do not resist” is treated as a comedian’s setup. | Capacity/release resistance is real. The source explicitly names the comic setup, so an unnamed specific comedy routine need not be invented. |
| Returned property is not restored agency, C3P0314–0443 | Companions complain that Kazuma has been returned in a broken state; the treasured gift becomes the actual cure. Megumin’s voluntary display adds a new relational response. | The gift’s function is unknown to Aqua at first. Serious restoration and possessive/rival attraction coexist; a cure does not retroactively justify earlier acts. |
| A threat defeats the speaker’s argument, C3P0524–0548; C4P0006–0137 | Darkness’s interrogation becomes reward through reflected pain; Kazuma’s solo-completion posture ends in death, familiar afterlife comedy and mediated bodily threats. | Reflection, death and threatened body interference remain real, but the exact edible object and full completed act are unshown. Eris reports the ground aftermath; she does not supply a stated observation mechanism. |
| Failed moral authority, C4P0234–0262 | Aqua condemns the laziness she shares, longs for the desperate debtor, and cannot sustain borrowed duty words under the others’ gaze. | The childish hiragana and fading phrase are exact written effects. Their whole-premise primary weight remains a genuine original mixed judgment; the broad hypocrisy is separately portable. |
| Stockpiled cure-all that nobody wanted, C4P0330–0346; FP0091–0114 | The confessed hoarder surveys the party and receives petty reasons for rejecting the quest; the shop’s powerful goods carry unacceptable costs or commercial bait. | The supplied survey sheet, its interviews and authorship are not independently verified. Fear of the very cold Neroido drink may invite a recognizable comedy echo, but a specific allusion is not source-certified. Asking for details at FP0114 is not a completed purchase. |
| Specialty pride and practical help share one destructive act, C4P0467–0520 | The reserve-ranked specialist destroys the shared recall letter, boasts first-team worth and then offers Yunyun excuses to stay; Kazuma’s accusation makes her flinch. | The first throw precedes the circulation warning; the later Explosion follows it. Friend support and status injury can coexist. Final town defense/stay and duty release are unshown. |
| Familiar teasing meets an actual attachment cost, FP0172–0373; EpP0003–0065 | Hunger prediction fails, moonlit beauty is punctured by familiar insults, a warning is instantly rejected, and a grand farewell ends in an erased request to be found. | Neither all-night confinement nor indefinite later insomnia is shown. Aqua’s written goal is real evidence of intent; her route, company, future fight and Kazuma’s completed pursuit are not. |

The H categories apply to claims rather than sanitize entire scenes. H1 covers literal selected acts, motives and consequences: assigning healing, resisting painful orders, paying for meals, unwanted exposure and a death followed by revival. H2 covers the amplified performance or magnitude of an otherwise causal pattern, such as a large public crowd or spectacular reciprocal pain. H3 limits stable generalization from gag-contingent performance or intensity; an actual body threat remains an act even where its threatened completion is unshown. H4 covers explicitly fictional, metaphorical or false content: the erotic passage, a wish-granting blue-tanuki comparison, the unverified grandiose billion-follower boast read as comic rhetoric, with Darkness’s divergent estimate kept distinct from an audited census, and false morning blame. A single unit can contain more than one level. The literal wrong done to a person survives alongside exaggeration and invented self-defense.
